The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #775800;">Gene E</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #775800;"><i>elephant</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #775800;">Gene E</span> cells absorb excessive amounts of liquid, resulting in giant, swollen cells.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7341a5;">Gene K</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #7341a5;"><i>knotted</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7341a5;">Gene K</span> cells grow in twisted, coiled shapes, resulting in a knotted or gnarled appearance.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #09697e;">Gene F</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #09697e;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #09697e;">Gene F</span> colonies are covered in soft, fine filaments, giving them a fuzzy, cotton-like texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #963564;">Gene N</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #963564;"><i>nude</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #963564;">Gene N</span> cells have an unusually smooth surface with no visible external features or textures.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #09697e;">Gene A</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #09697e;"><i>amber</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #09697e;">Gene A</span> cells develop a rich yellow-orange pigmentation, giving the colony a warm, amber hue.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #963564;">Gene W</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #963564;"><i>webbed</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #963564;">Gene W</span> colonies produce delicate, web-like strands that connect neighboring colonies in a cobweb pattern.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #64610e;">Gene P</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #64610e;"><i>pebble</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #64610e;">Gene P</span> produces colonies with a rough, uneven surface that resembles a collection of tiny pebbles.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006595;">Gene W</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #006595;"><i>webbed</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006595;">Gene W</span> colonies produce delicate, web-like strands that connect neighboring colonies in a cobweb pattern.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #775800;">Gene B</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #775800;"><i>bubbly</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #775800;">Gene B</span> produces excessive gas bubbles during growth, causing foamy appearance of the media.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #863d84;">Gene K</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #863d84;"><i>knotted</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #863d84;">Gene K</span> cells grow in twisted, coiled shapes, resulting in a knotted or gnarled appearance.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse;
